I need to hold at least 18 of the map's 24 villages (75%) for 2 consecutive turns, or eliminate every enemy village.

My villages:
[0] Lisboa: wood 30, iron 40. Troops at home: 2 spearman (out of 300). Available to send: 2 spearman.
[1] Santarem: wood 0, iron 0. Troops at home: 12 (5 spearman, 4 archer, 3 knight). Available to send: 5 spearman, 4 archer, 3 knight.
Wait, let's look at the resources:
Lisboa has wood 30 (+30/turn), iron 40 (+20/turn). So it can build something.
Santarem has wood 0 (+30/turn), iron 0 (+20/turn). It cannot build anything this turn because wood is 0 and iron is 0. But it produces 30 wood and 20 iron per turn, so next turn it will have 30 wood and 20 iron. Wait, resources belong to EACH village: a village pays for its own builds from its own stock.
So Santarem has 0 wood and 0 iron right now, so it cannot build anything this turn.
Lisboa has 30 wood and 40 iron. It can build:
- spearman: costs 15 wood + 0 iron. (Can build up to 2, but only 2 spearman are at home? No, cap is 300 troops. Lisboa has 2 troops at home, so it can build more).
Let's see: how many spearman can Lisboa build with 30 wood and 40 iron?
Each spearman costs 15 wood. 30 wood / 15 = 2 spearman.
Can it build archer? Archer costs 20 wood + 10 iron. With 30 wood and 40 iron, it can build 1 archer (costs 20 wood, 10 iron).
Can it build knight? Knight costs 30 wood + 30 iron. With 30 wood and 40 iron, it can build 1 knight (costs 30 wood, 30 iron).
Let's check the available troop types and quantities in Lisboa:
Currently at home: 2 spearman.
Let's build some troops in Lisboa. If we build 1 knight (30 wood, 30 iron), we'll have 1 knight and 2 spearman.
Or we can build 2 spearman (30 wood, 0 iron), so we'll have 4 spearman.
Or 1 archer (20 wood, 10 iron) and 1 spearman (15 wood, 0 iron) - total 35 wood, which is more than 30. So we can't do both.

What about 1 knight? 30 wood, 30 iron. Leaves 0 wood, 10 iron.

Let's evaluate the attacks on neutral villages:
Evora [2]: garrison 1 knight, defense 3.
If we attack with Lisboa's troops: we have 2 spearman. Attack power of 2 spearman is 2. Defense is 3. We would lose.
If we send reinforcements from Santarem to Lisboa? Santarem to Lisboa is 1 turn. But Lisboa is our capital, we can send troops to it. But if we send them, they will take 1 turn to arrive, and in the meantime Evora is attacked? No, we can attack Evora from Lisboa. But we need more troops.

Alternatively, we can attack Coimbra [3] or Badajoz [4] from Santarem.
Santarem has 5 spearman, 4 archer, 3 knight.
Let's calculate the attack power of Santarem's troops if we send them all:
Troops: 5 spearman (attack 1), 4 archer (attack 2), 3 knight (attack 4).
Total attack power: 5*1 + 4*2 + 3*4 = 5 + 8 + 12 = 25.
But wait! The most numerous type sets the counter matchup.
Here, spearman is 5, archer is 4, knight is 3. Most numerous is spearman (5).
Spearman counters knight, but is countered by archer.
Wait, let's read the combat rules carefully:
"Counter triangle (a BONUS, not an automatic win): spearman counters knight; archer counters spearman; knight counters archer. Having the counter multiplies your force by 1.5.
The MOST NUMEROUS type of each army sets the counter matchup (ties in count break in the order spearman, archer, knight)."
Wait, "each army" - does it mean the attacker's army and the defender's army separately?
"The MOST NUMEROUS type of each army sets the counter matchup (ties in count break in the order spearman, archer, knight)."
Usually, this means:
For the attacker, the most numerous type in the attacker's army determines the attacker's "counter type".
For the defender, the most numerous type in the defender's army determines the defender's "counter type".
Let's see: if attacker's most numerous is spearman, and defender's most numerous is knight, then spearman counters knight, so attacker gets a 1.5x multiplier.
If attacker's most numerous is spearman, and defender's most numerous is archer, archer counters spearman, so defender gets a 1.5x multiplier? Or does the defender's counter type apply to their defense?
"Having the counter multiplies your force by 1.5."
So if the attacker's most numerous type counters the defender's most numerous type, the attacker's force is multiplied by 1.5.
If the defender's most numerous type counters the attacker's most numerous type, the defender's force is multiplied by 1.5.
Let's check Coimbra [3]: garrison is 1 archer.
Defender's army: 1 archer. Most numerous is archer.
Attacker's army (Santarem): 5 spearman, 4 archer, 3 knight. Most numerous is spearman (5).
Archer counters spearman. So the defender (Coimbra) has the counter! The defender's force is multiplied by 1.5.
Wait, defender's effective defense in Coimbra is 3 (location bonus included).
If defender has the counter, does the 1.5 multiplier apply to the effective defense?
"The 'effective defense' values in the report ALREADY include the location bonus. Use them directly; do not apply the bonus again."
But the counter multiplier is separate from the location bonus.
"Having the counter multiplies your force by 1.5."
So if defender has the counter, their effective defense of 3 is multiplied by 1.5, making it 4.5.
Attacker's force: 25. 25 vs 4.5, attacker wins easily.
Wait, let's look at Badajoz [4]: garrison 2 archers, defense 5.
Defender's army: 2 archers. Most numerous is archer.
Attacker's army: spearman (5). Archer counters spearman, so defender has the counter. Defender's force is 5 * 1.5 = 7.5.
Attacker's force is 25. Attacker wins easily.

What about Evora [2]? Garrison 1 knight, defense 3.
If we attack from Lisboa with only 2 spearman:
Attacker's army: 2 spearman. Most numerous is spearman.
Defender's army: 1 knight. Most numerous is knight.
Spearman counters knight, so attacker has the counter!
Attacker's force: 2 spearman * 1.5 = 3.
Defender's defense: 3.
Since 3 vs 3 is a tie, and "A tie favors the DEFENDER", the defender wins.
So we cannot take Evora with just 2 spearman.

"plan": "<your note to your next turn, 2 to 4 lines (anything past 600 characters is cut off). You will read it next turn. Write what you are trying to do, what you must not forget, and what you decided NOT to do. It is a note to yourself: be useful, not eloquent."
"statement": "<2-4 lines for the audience>"

Let's look at the combat rules again: "When you ATTACK, your troops count their ATTACK; the DEFENDER counts the DEFENSE of theirs. A tie favors the DEFENDER."

So the steps:
1. Attacker sums attack of all troops. Defender sums defense of all troops.
2. Determine most numerous type for each army. If tie, use order spearman, archer, knight to break.
3. Check if one side's most numerous type counters the other's. If so, that side gets 1.5x multiplier on their force (attack or defense).
4. Apply location bonus to defender: defense x1.25 in village, x1.5 in castle. (These are already included in the "effective defense" given in the report, so we don't apply again.)
5. Compare effective forces. Higher wins. Tie favors defender.

Attack of spearman is 1, knight is 4.
Total attack = 4 * 1 * 1.5 + 1 * 4 = 6 + 4 = 10?
Wait, how is the counter triangle calculated?
"Counter triangle (a BONUS, not an automatic win): spearman counters knight; archer counters spearman; knight counters archer. Having the counter multiplies your force by 1.5."
"The MOST NUMEROUS type of each army sets the counter matchup (ties in count break in the order spearman, archer, knight)."
So the most numerous type is spearman (4). The defender has 4 knights (most numerous type is knight).
Spearman counters knight. So the attacker's force is multiplied by 1.5.
Attacker's force: spearman attack 1, knight attack 4.
Is it (4 * 1 + 1 * 4) * 1.5 = 8 * 1.5 = 12?
Or does it only multiply the spearman part? "multiplies your force by 1.5" usually means the whole force, or the attacking force. Let's assume it multiplies the whole attacking force, or the relevant part. Either way, the effective defense of Salamanca is 10.
If attack is 12 (or 10), it might win, but let's see. The army is arriving next turn.

To attack Cordoba with the combined force, we must NOT send the garrison of Sevilha this turn. Instead, we keep them in Sevilha, let the reinforcements arrive at Sevilha, and then next turn we send the entire combined force from Sevilha to Cordoba.

Let's re-read the combat rules section carefully:

"=== COMBAT RULES ===
Each troop type has its own ATTACK and DEFENSE values (they are not equal):
  spearman: attack 1, defense 2, speed slow (costs 15 wood + 0 iron).
  archer: attack 2, defense 2, speed medium (costs 20 wood + 10 iron).
  knight: attack 4, defense 2, speed fast (costs 30 wood + 30 iron).
When you ATTACK, your troops count their ATTACK; the DEFENDER counts the DEFENSE of theirs.
Counter triangle (a BONUS, not an automatic win): spearman counters knight; archer counts spearman; knight counts archer. Having the counter multiplies your WHOLE army's force by 1.5 (every troop in it, not only the countering type).
The MOST NUMEROUS type of each army sets the counter matchup (ties in count break in the order spearman, archer, knight).
Defending is easier: defense counts x1.25 in a village and x1.5 in a castle (capital). In the open field (on a road) there is no bonus.
The side with the HIGHER effective force wins. A tie favors the DEFENDER.
The LOSER is destroyed entirely - this includes an attacker that fails against a village. The WINNER loses troops worth HALF of the loser's effective force, spread over its troop types: that amount does not depend on the winner's size, so a bigger winning army loses a smaller share of itself. A defender that holds its village loses troops the same way.
The "effective defense" values in the report ALREADY include the location bonus. Use them directly; do not apply the bonus again."

So the "effective defense" given in the report is the defender's base defense multiplied by the location bonus (x1.25 for village, x1.5 for castle). That number is what we see. But it does NOT include the counter bonus. The counter bonus is a separate multiplier that applies to the whole army's force (attack for attacker, defense for defender) if that army's most numerous type counters the opponent's most numerous type.

So when we attack, we need to calculate our effective attack = (sum of our troops' attack) * (1.5 if our most numerous type counters their most numerous type, else 1). The defender's effective defense = (the number given in the report) * (1.5 if their most numerous type counters our most numerous type, else 1). Then compare.
